to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 17.50002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-1.51042pt\raise 9.55482pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 17.50002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 17.50002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}\kern 12.04329pt}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}}\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N},\tau\Rightarrow\tau$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 5.46104pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to100.1143pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 31.92625pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N},\tau\Rightarrow\tau$}}\kern 26.46521pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 33.93599pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{rec}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to141.27254pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 26.46521pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 84.55498pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\sigma},N\Rightarrow\tau$}}\kern 77.0842pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

We can verify that the derivation after transformation is provably equivalent in
\mathit{T}
by an object-level induction on the recursion parameter:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}\,0\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}\,\mathsf{s}\,t)\,\vec{x}\,0&=&\mathsf{cut}\,0\,s\,\vec{x}&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
&=&s\,\vec{x}\,0&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}$ axiom}\\
&=&\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}\,0\,0&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
&=&\mathsf{cntr}\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t)\,\vec{x}\,0&\text{by $\mathsf{cntr}$ axiom}\\
\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt\cr\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}\,0\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}\,\mathsf{s}\,t)\,\vec{x}\,\mathsf{s}y&=&\mathsf{cut}\,\mathsf{s}\,t\,\vec{x}\,y\,(\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}\,0\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}\,\mathsf{s}\,t)\,\vec{x}\,y)&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
&=&\mathsf{cut}\,\mathsf{s}\,t\,\vec{x}\,y\,(\mathsf{cntr}\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t)\,\vec{x}\,y)&\text{by inductive hypothesis}\\
&=&t\,\vec{x}\,\mathsf{s}y\,y\,(\mathsf{cntr}\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t)\,\vec{x}\,y)&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}$ axiom}\\
&=&\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}\,\mathsf{s}y\,\mathsf{s}y&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
&=&\mathsf{cntr}\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t)\,\vec{x}\,\mathsf{s}y&\text{by $\mathsf{cntr}$ axiom}\qed\end{array}

We shall also use a generalised version of the cut rule that incorporates both context sharing and context splitting behaviour:

(32)

{{}{}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 14.73814pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau$}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau$}}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to97.91539pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 22.64056pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\kern 22.64056pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\qquad\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\ =\ t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})

The point of this rule is to absorb extraneous weakening steps from cut-reductions into cuts.
Note that we have been implicitly using this until now, e.g. as in the derivation in the proof sketch of Proposition
104
.
Similarly to
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
, the rule
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
and its corresponding equation can be derived from
\mathsf{cut}
and
\mathsf{wk}
:

{{}{}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 1.04997pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 10.98329pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.8889pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}}\kern 3.8889pt}\kern 1.43518pt\kern-0.7pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 1.8pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to1.8pt{\vfill\hbox to46.60995pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.0pt\hbox to46.60995pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 1.8pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\kern-0.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{0},\underline{\vec{\rho}_{1}},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\kern 10.98329pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 10.35703pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau$}}}\kern 10.35703pt}\kern 1.43518pt\kern-0.7pt\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 1.8pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to1.8pt{\vfill\hbox to58.20361pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.0pt\hbox to58.20361pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 1.8pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}}\kern 1.43518pt\kern-0.7pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\underline{\vec{\rho}_{0}},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau$}\kern 0.0pt$}}}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 10.98329pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to125.79688pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 47.56459pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma$}}\kern 36.5813pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\qquad\begin{array}[]{rl}&\mathsf{cut}\,(\mathsf{wk}^{*}s)\,(\mathsf{wk}^{*}t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\\
=&\mathsf{wk}^{*}t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}(\mathsf{wk}^{*}s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y})\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(\mathsf{wk}^{*}s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y})\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})\end{array}

Again, we may freely use
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
in derivations, understanding it to be shorthand for the derivation above.
However, crucially, we will only count instances of
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
as single steps, in order to facilitate the upcoming induction arguments.
26
26

26

Another approach could be to ignore all
\mathsf{wk}
and
\mathsf{cntr}
steps (‘weak inferences’) when counting the size/depth of derivations, but then we would need a lemma for commuting above those steps in order to reduce an induction measure based on size/depth.

A.2.
Main free-cut elimination argument

As usual, our overall argument will be by an induction on the complexity of cut-formulas:

•

The
level
of a
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
on
\sigma
is just
\mathsf{lev}(\sigma)
.

•

The
d
-level of a derivation is the multiset of all its cut-levels
>d
.

We assume that multisets of natural numbers are ordered in the usual way.
27
27

27

It is well-known that for any well-order
<
of order type
\alpha
, the corresponding multiset order has order type
\omega^{\alpha}
. Thus the multiset order on even
\omega
is already not available in
\mathsf{RCA}_{0}
, whose proof-theoretic ordinal is
\omega^{\omega}
.
However, cut-elimination arguments based on the multiset ordering are nonetheless typically formalisable in
\mathsf{RCA}_{0}
thanks to explicit complexity bounds on the multiset branching, i.e. the number of smaller number occurrences that replace a larger one when reducing.

We may write
\mathsf{cut}_{d}^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{cut}_{\leq d}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{cut}_{>d}^{\prime}
for a
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
instance of level
d
,
\leq d
or
>d
respectively.

Lemma 105
.

Let
t:\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau
be a contraction-free
\mathit{T}_{n}
-derivation with at least one
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}
. Then there is a contraction-free
\mathit{T}_{n}
-derivation
t^{\prime}:\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau
of lower
n
-level such that
\mathit{T}_{n}\vdash t=t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
|t|
, by inspection of a topmost
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}
.
All permutation cases are given in the subsequent subsections, as well as arguments verifying that the derivations before and after transformation are provably equivalent in
\mathit{T}_{n}
.
In each case, we may have to apply the induction hypothesis zero, one or two times to smaller subderivations.
Note that the only transformation that introduces more cuts (even after applying inductive hypotheses) is the
\mathsf{R}
-
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
‘key’ case.
∎

Proposition
16
is now an immediate consequence of the following result:

Theorem 106
.

For any
\mathit{T}_{n}
-derivation
t:\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau
there is a
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}
-free
\mathit{T}_{n}
-derivation
t^{\prime}:\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau
, such that
\mathit{T}\vdash t^{\prime}\vec{x}\ =\ t\,\vec{x}
.

Proof.

Assume
t
is contraction-free by Proposition
104
.
The result follows by induction on the
n
-level of
t
, applying Lemma
105
above for the inductive steps.
∎

A.3.
\mathsf{R}
-
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
key case

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\sigma

\scriptstyle\mathsf{R}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s^{\prime}}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\sigma

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s^{\prime}}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

where we write
s^{\prime}
for the derivation

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\sigma

\scriptstyle\mathsf{R}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\sigma

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{cut}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}r)\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}r)\,s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})))&\text{by $\mathsf{R}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{L}\,r\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s^{\prime}\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{L}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s^{\prime}(\mathsf{L}\,r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}

A.4.
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{rec}
commutative case

The most interesting commutative case is commuting a
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}
above a
\mathsf{rec}_{\leq n}
step:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{x}_{0},\vec{\sigma},N\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma,N,\tau\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{rec}_{\tau}\;

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma,N\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},N\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to,

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{x}_{0},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N}\Rightarrow\tau

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{x}_{0},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N},\tau\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N},\tau\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{rec}_{\tau}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}N},\underline{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N}}}\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cntr}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\underline{N}\Rightarrow\tau

where we have underlined principal types and used colours to identify type occurrences according to ancestry.

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows.
First we show that,

(33)

\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,z\ =\ \mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,z

by (object-level) induction on
z
.
For the base case:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,0&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
=&s\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,0&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\end{array}

For the inductive step:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,\mathsf{s}z&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,z\,(\mathsf{rec}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,z)&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,a\,z\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,z)&\text{by inductive hypothesis, \eqref{eqn:comm-cut-rec-ih}}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,z\,(\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,z)&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{rec}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,a)\,\mathsf{s}z&\text{by $\mathsf{rec}$ axioms}\end{array}

From here we conclude the verification in
\mathit{T}_{n}
by the
\mathsf{cntr}
and
\mathsf{cut}
axioms.

A.5.
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{cut}
commutative cases

Sometimes we have to commute a
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}
over a
\mathsf{cut}_{\leq n}
.
It is notationally cumbersome to consider all splitting possibilities for commuting
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
over
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
, so instead we just permute a
\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}
step over a
\mathsf{cut}_{\leq n}
step.
From here, commutation over a
\mathsf{cut}_{\leq n}^{\prime}
step follows by further commutation over
\mathsf{wk}
-steps (see next subsection).

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\sigma

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}_{\leq n}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}_{>n}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}_{\leq n}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(\mathsf{cut}\,r\,s\,\vec{x}_{0},\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}(\mathsf{cut}\,r\,s)\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}

There is also a similar cut-commutation the other way around, when the right premiss of a
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
step ends with
\mathsf{cut}
.
There is no commutation of
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
above the left side of a
\mathsf{rec}
step, since that would immediately imply that the level of the cut is bounded by that of a recursor.

A.6.
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{wk}
key and commutative case

There are two possible interactions between
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{wk}
, depending on whether the cut-formula is weakened or not. Both are relatively simple.

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{wk}^{*}t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axioms}\\
=&\mathsf{wk}\,t\,\vec{x}_{1},\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,(\mathsf{wk}\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1},\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;

\rho,\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\rho,\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to either the left or right derivations below, depending on whether the purple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho}
is present or not, respectively:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\rho,\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\rho,\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\tau

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation, respectively, as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{wk}\,t\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,(\mathsf{wk}\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}\qquad\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{wk}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\,s\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\,s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{wk}\,t\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,(\mathsf{wk}\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,x\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}

There are similar cases when the left premiss of a
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
concludes a
\mathsf{wk}
step.

A.7.
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
commutative cases

We have to treat the cases when a
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}
step is on the left or on the right separately.

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\sigma,\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\pi

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\pi

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma},\pi,\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to,

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\scriptstyle\mathsf{wk}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\sigma,\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\pi

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma},\pi\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

where the purple occurrence of
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}
after transformation is present only if it is present before transformation.

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows,

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{wk}^{*}r)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(\mathsf{wk}^{*}r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z))&\text{by $\mathsf{L}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,z))&\text{by $\mathsf{wk}$ axioms}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,z)))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z}\,(\mathsf{L}^{\prime}r\,s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,z)&\text{by $\mathsf{L}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}(\mathsf{L}^{\prime}r\,s)\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\end{array}

where, again, the purple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z}
is present just if the purple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}
are present in the derivations.

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}\Rightarrow\pi

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\pi\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma,\pi\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\pi\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

is transformed to,

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}\Rightarrow\pi

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\pi\Rightarrow\rho

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\rho

\textstyle{\scriptstyle r}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}\Rightarrow\pi

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma,\pi\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma,\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\to\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

where the purple occurrences of
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}
after transformation are present just if they are before transformation.

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows,

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{L}^{\prime}(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s)\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z))&\text{by $\mathsf{L}^{\prime}$ axiom }\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(s\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z})))&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(z\,(s\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z})))\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z})&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{L}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z\,(r\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y}\,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z})&\text{by $\mathsf{L}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}r\,(\mathsf{L}^{\prime}s\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}

where the purple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}z}
occurrences are present only if the purple
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rho\to\sigma}
occurrences are present in the derivations.

A.8.
\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{R}
commutative case

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma,\rho\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{R}\;

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma\Rightarrow\rho\to\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\tau

is transformed to:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle s}

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\sigma

\textstyle{\scriptstyle t}

\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\sigma,\rho\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma},\rho\Rightarrow\tau

\scriptstyle\mathsf{R}\;

\vec{\rho}_{0},\vec{\rho}_{1},\vec{\sigma}\Rightarrow\rho\to\tau

We verify that
\mathit{T}_{n}
proves the equality of the derivations before and after transformation as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\mathsf{R}\,(\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t)\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}s\,t\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\text{by $\mathsf{R}$ axiom}\\
=&t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})\,z&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{R}\,t\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,(s\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{y})\,z&\text{by $\mathsf{R}$ axiom}\\
=&\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}\,s\,(\mathsf{R}\,t)\,\vec{x}_{0}\,\vec{x}_{1}\,\vec{y}\,z&\text{by $\mathsf{cut}^{\prime}$ axiom}\end{array}
